# Denmark content refresh — research and claim audit (Part 1)

**As of:** 17 November 2026
**Scope:** DK-TP-01, DK-TP-02, DK-EMP-01, DK-EMP-02; UUID/title mapping for all eight Denmark articles.
**Editorial status:** research and staging only. No canonical/production records changed.

## A. Scope and mapping

Part 1 covers only temporary-status and employment articles. Part 2 will handle health, housing, education, social assistance, full Compare review and country-wide reconciliation.

## B–G. Counts and complete Part-1 ledger

Ledger methodology: every factual English sentence in each scoped article is recorded as a research ledger row, with article-local IDs. This statement-level ledger preserves coordinated legal conditions and exceptions; it does not use the historical triage count of 485 as a target.

- Existing claims reviewed: 183
- New important claims: 4
- Total ledger entries: 187
- CONFIRMED: 176
- UPDATED: 7
- INCORRECT: 0
- NO LONGER SUPPORTED: 0
- QUALIFICATION REQUIRED: 0
- NEW IMPORTANT INFORMATION: 4
- Reconciliation: 187 statuses = 187 ledger entries.

| DK-TP-01-C001 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | Denmark’s Ukraine Special Act is a national scheme. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C002 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | Denmark does not participate in the EU Temporary Protection Directive. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C003 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | A protection permit or status from another EU country does not by itself establish eligibility under Danish law. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C004 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | The [[source:486ee6fc-fc67-4d04-891b-acb967e5477b/Danish Immigration Service]] says a person may qualify under section 1 if, for example, they are a Ukrainian citizen who left Ukraine on or after 1 February 2022 and had residence there on or after that date; a person recognised as a refugee in Ukraine on 24 February 2022 who left Ukraine on or after 1 February 2022; or a Ukrainian citizen or such a recognised refugee who was in Denmark or held Danish residence permission on 24 February 2022. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C005 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | The agency says some people who lived elsewhere and did not return to Ukraine before leaving may normally not meet the section 1 departure condition. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C006 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | A non-Ukrainian may have a route if they were recognised as a refugee in Ukraine on the relevant date, or may qualify as a close family member under section 2. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C007 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | Other citizenship or a residence permit in another country can affect the assessment. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C008 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | These rules are fact-specific; the list is not a decision about any reader’s eligibility. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C009 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | [[source:814a05cf-08c2-4f0d-b516-4ff3655f5b3c/Special rules]] apply to Ukrainian men aged 23–59 who are subject to Ukraine’s mobilisation rules. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |

| DK-TP-01-C012 | DK-TP-01 — Who can apply | The agency says a permit granted before 3 September 2026 on an application submitted on or after 25 June 2026 may be revoked unless this would conflict with Denmark’s international obligations; the person must be contacted and given an opportunity to comment and provide information before a decision. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |

| DK-TP-01-C071 | DK-TP-01 — Where to get help | For current eligibility, application forms, appointments, and case questions, use the Danish Immigration Service’s “New to Denmark” Ukraine pages. - For a section 2 family application, use the official family-member page and SL2 instructions. - For address registration and CPR, ask the municipality or International Citizen Service that handles your address, and consult Life in Denmark. - For healthcare, housing, and social assistance after approval, see the separate Denmark guides; confirm case-specific requirements with the responsible authority. - Before relying on a general extension date, ask the Danish Immigration Service to confirm the date in your own decision or written case communication. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C072 | DK-TP-01 — Where to get help | This draft is general information, not individual immigration or legal advice. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-01-C073 | DK-TP-01 — Where to get help | It does not recommend which application a person should file. | CONFIRMED | DIS application, waiting, family and extension guidance; BEK 828/2026; Act 324/2022 as amended; L 43 status | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-02-C001 | DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates | A residence permit under Denmark’s Ukraine Special Act for people displaced from Ukraine is temporary. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-02-C002 | DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates | It does not automatically establish which other permits a person may qualify for. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-02-C003 | DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates | 828/2026, in force from 1 October 2026, provides for qualifying permits to be extended through 17 March 2028 unless revocation grounds under section 10 apply. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-02-C004 | DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates | The Danish Immigration Service assesses extension on its own initiative; holders do not normally submit an individual application. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-02-C005 | DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates | Extension depends on continuing to meet the requirements, including residing in Denmark and the underlying permit basis remaining valid. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-02-C006 | DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates | For a section 2 spouse or cohabiting partner, continued cohabitation with the section 1 permit holder is generally required, subject to the exception for a partner who has gone to Ukraine for a commendable purpose. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-02-C007 | DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates | The Immigration Service’s update of 29 October 2026 explains that existing cards may still show validity through 17 March 2027 while extension cases are processed. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-02-C008 | DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates | If no decision has been made by that date, the holder may remain in Denmark with the same rights until the extension process is complete. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |

| DK-TP-02-C010 | DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates | The older 17 March 2027 date describes current card validity during processing; it is not an unresolved conflict with the 2028 extension. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |

| DK-TP-02-C014 | DK-TP-02 — Employment-related residence options | at least two years of legal, continuous employment with the same employer without state or municipal wage subsidies paid to the employer; - pay and terms corresponding to Danish standards; - generally, full-time work; in some industries, fewer than 37 hours per week may count as full time. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |
| DK-TP-02-C015 | DK-TP-02 — Employment-related residence options | Further requirements apply, and applying does not guarantee approval. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |

| DK-TP-02-C022 | DK-TP-02 — Other residence permits | For example, the [[source:6dd24d38-f2a3-4703-b864-5caf4db2baef/Pay Limit Scheme]] requires a job offer that meets that scheme’s conditions, while an ordinary higher-education permit requires admission and full-time study. | CONFIRMED | SIRI Labour Market Attachment, Educational Attachment, Pay Limit Scheme; DIS; permanent residence guidance | See numeric/date audit; otherwise current as of 17 Nov 2026 | Retain in candidate. |

## H.–N. Findings

### Temporary status, EU framework and eligibility

Denmark’s displaced-person route is the national Ukraine Special Act (Act no. 324/2022, as amended), not an EU Temporary Protection Directive permit. Denmark’s justice/home-affairs opt-out means Directive 2001/55/EC does not automatically govern this national permit; a permit from another EU country does not itself establish Danish eligibility. Principal §1 routes include specified Ukrainian citizens and people recognized as refugees in Ukraine, subject to exact residence/departure dates and conditions. §2 is a separate family-member route; it must not be collapsed into §1. L 43 was a bill, not law, as of 17 November 2026.

### Military-age condition

For Ukrainian men aged 23–59 who are subject to Ukrainian mobilization rules, current DIS guidance requires proof of fulfilment of military obligations and exemption. The application-date trigger is 25 June 2026; the amendment took effect 3 September 2026. A permit granted before 3 September on a post-25 June application may be revoked unless international obligations prevent it; the person must be heard before a decision. Pre-25 June applications/permits are grandfathered, including later extension applications. Under-23 permits may be limited to the point the person becomes subject to mobilization and cannot be extended without required proof. This does not apply indiscriminately to all Ukrainian men or all permit holders. Rule applies to §1 and §2; official proof is from Ukrainian authorities and current-status documents; DIS recommends Rezerv+/Diia.

### Extension to March 2028

BEK no. 828/2026 took effect 1 October 2026 and authorizes qualifying extensions through 17 March 2028, subject to §10 revocation grounds. DIS’s 29 October 2026 notice states no individual extension application is required: the authority assesses on its own initiative and extends if conditions remain met. Affected holders must continue to reside in Denmark and retain the underlying basis. A §2 spouse/cohabiting partner generally must continue living with the §1 holder, with an exception for a partner who left for Ukraine for a commendable purpose. Existing cards may show 17 March 2027 while processing; if undecided by then, the holder may stay with same rights until the extension decision; a new card follows without fresh biometrics. Therefore the apparent date conflict is resolved by the later operational notice: card/current printed validity date vs extended validity and pending-processing rights.

### Application and pending rights

§1 filing requires presence in Denmark; §2 family applications can be filed in or outside Denmark. The Danish Immigration Service handles the route. SL1 is the principal form, digital or paper; SL2 is distinct for family. Current guidance says no fee, identity evidence, biometrics within four weeks (with listed exception), receipt when DIS receives the application, and 35-day average target (not guarantee). The “waiting” guidance is primarily framed for applicants in Denmark. Pending presence is not approval. Work can start after biometrics only if it is not obvious the applicant cannot qualify; DIS advises against work where eligibility is uncertain. Some healthcare access and optional asylum-centre accommodation are described; do not infer general benefits, ordinary healthcare, or unconditional travel/re-entry rights.

### Family and ordinary residence

§2 covers defined close relatives and requires family life to have existed on 24 February 2022; evidence and categories are individually assessed. Family route has separate filing form and may be filed abroad. It is not identical to §1; §2 work rights after grant and the military-proof rule are stated separately. Special Act status does not automatically convert to another permit. Labour Market Attachment requires at least two years of continuous lawful work for the same employer without public support, Danish-standard pay/conditions, generally full-time work, and no self-employment; other schemes (education, Pay Limit) have their own thresholds. Special Act residence alone is not a qualifying basis for permanent residence; general residence-period and permit-type conditions apply.

### Employment

Granted Special Act permit holders may work subject to their permit. Pending work is separate and conditional. Employer must check foreign worker work rights. Act 501/2023 written-terms threshold: average more than three hours weekly over four consecutive weeks (including no-guaranteed-hours work); aggregation and statutory/collective-agreement exceptions apply. Seven-day and one-month periods are delivery deadlines for specified written terms, not work eligibility periods. Denmark has no statutory nationwide minimum wage; wages generally derive from collective agreement or individual negotiation, with scheme-specific immigration salary thresholds distinct. Thirty-seven hours is common in many full-time jobs, not a universal statutory week. Five weeks general holiday entitlement, sick pay and supplements depend on legal category/contract/agreement. A-kasse and trade-union membership are voluntary; unemployment benefit eligibility is separate. SKAT tax card and CPR/personal tax numbers are administrative records, not residence or work authorization; no claim that CPR/MitID/NemKonto are issued automatically. Employment does not imply self-employment permission.

## O. Numeric/date audit

Numeric/date value-bearing sentences audited: 42/42 (EN candidate copies; the aligned locale copies were separately compared). Every such assertion maps to its EN ledger sentence. Audit entries follow.

- **DK-TP-01 — Who can apply:** The [[source:486ee6fc-fc67-4d04-891b-acb967e5477b|Danish Immigration Service]] says a person may qualify under section 1 if, for example, they are a Ukrainian citizen who left Ukraine on or after 1 February 2022 and had residence there on or after that date; a person recognised as a refugee in Ukraine on 24 February 2022 who left Ukraine on or after 1 February 2022; or a Ukrainian citizen or such a recognised refugee who was in Denmark or held Danish residence permission on 24 February 2022.
- **DK-TP-01 — Who can apply:** The agency says some people who lived elsewhere and did not return to Ukraine before leaving may normally not meet the section 1 departure condition.
- **DK-TP-01 — Who can apply:** A non-Ukrainian may have a route if they were recognised as a refugee in Ukraine on the relevant date, or may qualify as a close family member under section 2.
- **DK-TP-01 — Who can apply:** [[source:814a05cf-08c2-4f0d-b516-4ff3655f5b3c|Special rules]] apply to Ukrainian men aged 23–59 who are subject to Ukraine’s mobilisation rules.
- **DK-TP-01 — Who can apply:** This rule applies to applications submitted on or after 25 June 2026.
- **DK-TP-01 — Who can apply:** The agency says a permit granted before 3 September 2026 on an application submitted on or after 25 June 2026 may be revoked unless this would conflict with Denmark’s international obligations; the person must be contacted and given an opportunity to comment and provide information before a decision.
- **DK-TP-01 — Who can apply:** The amendment does not affect people whose permit was already granted on an application submitted before 25 June 2026, including later extension applications.
- **DK-TP-01 — Who can apply:** Some permits for people under 23 may be limited until they become subject to mobilisation rules.
- **DK-TP-01 — How to submit an application:** For a section 1 application, the Immigration Service says you must be in Denmark.
- **DK-TP-01 — How to submit an application:** A qualifying section 2 family application may be submitted in Denmark or abroad.
- **DK-TP-01 — How to submit an application:** The main applicant uses form [[source:9d53a568-07c9-4713-a5e8-1c4db5826a98|SL1]].
- **DK-TP-01 — How to submit an application:** Family members who use section 2 have a separate SL2 form and requirements.
- **DK-TP-01 — How to submit an application:** It currently gives an average maximum processing-time target of 35 days; this is not a promise, and some cases take longer.
- **DK-TP-01 — While waiting for a decision:** The Immigration Service says it begins after your fingerprints and facial image have been recorded, and does not apply if you obviously cannot qualify under section 1 or 2.
- **DK-TP-01 — What happens after approval:** Under section 57(5) of the Integration Act, ordinary section 12 accommodation placement does not apply to a permit under section 2(1) of the Ukraine Special Act, although the Danish Immigration Service may decide otherwise in very special personal circumstances.
- **DK-TP-01 — Extension and permit validity:** 828/2026, in force from 1 October 2026, the Danish Immigration Service may extend qualifying Ukraine Special Act permits to 17 March 2028, subject to the revocation grounds in section 10.
- **DK-TP-01 — Extension and permit validity:** For a section 2 spouse or cohabiting partner, continued cohabitation with the section 1 permit holder is generally required, subject to the stated exception where the partner has gone to Ukraine for a commendable purpose.
- **DK-TP-01 — Extension and permit validity:** The Immigration Service’s 29 October 2026 update explains that existing permit cards may still show validity through 17 March 2027 while extension cases are processed.
- **DK-TP-01 — Extension and permit validity:** The old public page date is not an unresolved legal conflict after the 29 October update.
- **DK-TP-01 — Family members and special circumstances:** Section 2 may cover specified close family members of a person with a section 1 permit, including a cohabiting spouse or partner, an unmarried child under 18, and certain dependent or otherwise qualifying close relatives.
- **DK-TP-01 — Family members and special circumstances:** The agency says the relevant family life must have existed on 24 February 2022.
- **DK-TP-01 — Where to get help:** For current eligibility, application forms, appointments, and case questions, use the Danish Immigration Service’s “New to Denmark” Ukraine pages. - For a section 2 family application, use the official family-member page and SL2 instructions. - For address registration and CPR, ask the municipality or International Citizen Service that handles your address, and consult Life in Denmark. - For healthcare, housing, and social assistance after approval, see the separate Denmark guides; confirm case-specific requirements with the responsible authority. - Before relying on a general extension date, ask the Danish Immigration Service to confirm the date in your own decision or written case communication.
- **DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates:** 828/2026, in force from 1 October 2026, provides for qualifying permits to be extended through 17 March 2028 unless revocation grounds under section 10 apply.
- **DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates:** For a section 2 spouse or cohabiting partner, continued cohabitation with the section 1 permit holder is generally required, subject to the exception for a partner who has gone to Ukraine for a commendable purpose.
- **DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates:** The Immigration Service’s update of 29 October 2026 explains that existing cards may still show validity through 17 March 2027 while extension cases are processed.
- **DK-TP-02 — Temporary residence and extension dates:** The older 17 March 2027 date describes current card validity during processing; it is not an unresolved conflict with the 2028 extension.
- **DK-TP-02 — Employment-related residence options:** The [[source:4b278bd5-dcc7-4a9d-96eb-ecf20719d9d3|Labour Market Attachment Scheme]] describes a separate route for a person with a valid residence and work permit under the Special Act.
- **DK-TP-02 — Employment-related residence options:** at least two years of legal, continuous employment with the same employer without state or municipal wage subsidies paid to the employer; - pay and terms corresponding to Danish standards; - generally, full-time work; in some industries, fewer than 37 hours per week may count as full time.
- **DK-TP-02 — Education-related residence options:** The [[source:adac4bdc-17cb-459f-b675-b6581995c1f3|Educational Attachment Scheme]] describes a separate route for holders of a valid Special Act permit.
- **DK-TP-02 — Other residence permits:** For example, the [[source:6dd24d38-f2a3-4703-b864-5caf4db2baef|Pay Limit Scheme]] requires a job offer that meets that scheme’s conditions, while an ordinary higher-education permit requires admission and full-time study.
- **DK-TP-02 — Permanent residence:** Its page describes a general residence-duration requirement of at least eight years (four in certain situations) and requires specified permit types during the relevant period.
- **DK-EMP-01 — Opening:** You can search for vacancies on [[source:fffe5fdb-0c81-47fc-9539-6bcc90949435|Workindenmark’s job portal]].
- **DK-EMP-01 — Opening:** On [[source:b9577c82-6be2-4a73-a159-fcaa85968b8e|Jobnet]], you can use the Find Job function without logging in; its guide is in Danish.
- **DK-EMP-02 — Who can work in Denmark:** If you have been granted a residence permit under the Ukraine Special Act, [[source:b0ceabf9-2608-4748-86f7-e5e826eb6641|the Danish Immigration Service]] says you are entitled to work while that permit is valid.
- **DK-EMP-02 — Who can work in Denmark:** Section 1 and section 2 have different eligibility conditions.
- **DK-EMP-02 — Who can work in Denmark:** This does not apply if you obviously cannot qualify under section 1 or 2.
- **DK-EMP-02 — Employment contracts:** The [[source:2acf95dd-1fea-45bc-9478-fda84be4496d|written-information rules]] generally cover employees whose predetermined or actual working time averages more than 3 hours a week over 4 consecutive weeks.
- **DK-EMP-02 — Employment contracts:** Some specified information is due within 7 calendar days after employment starts; other information is due within one month.
- **DK-EMP-02 — Salary and working hours:** Around 37 hours a week is common in many full-time jobs, but it is not a universal statutory working week.
- **DK-EMP-02 — Holidays, sickness and working conditions:** The general statutory holiday entitlement is five weeks, but how holiday pay is provided and how much you have accrued depend on your employment and pay arrangement.
- **DK-EMP-02 — Taxes and registration:** If you start a job, contact the Danish Tax Agency (SKAT) about [[source:dd5b7430-e9ae-4f48-9e14-3a511ae5fc96|a tax card]] and the personal tax-number process.
- **DK-EMP-02 — Workplace rights and where to raise a concern:** It accepts [[source:25cfdf97-36ca-4f82-8b61-85089d022079|anonymous complaints about suspected violations]] and certain abusive behaviour.
## P. Translation alignment

EN is the factual master. The extension section was updated in all three languages. Review copies present 12 locale versions (4/4 articles).

## Q. Cross-article consistency

TP-01/TP-02 now distinguish extension of the current permit from new ordinary-residence routes. TP-01/EMP-02 distinguish granted work rights from conditional pending work. EMP-01 job-search guidance makes no immigration authorization claim. Employment and self-employment remain separate. Unresolved material contradictions in the Part-1 candidate set: 0. Unresolved material evidence conflicts preventing safe wording: 0.

## S. Compare impact candidates

1. Temporary-protection duration/expiry — likely REVISE: current meaning distinguishes 17 March 2027 card date, pending same-rights period, and extension through 17 March 2028.
2. Temporary-protection eligibility — likely REVISE: military-age eligibility and L 43 must be reflected in any comparison only when enacted/current; L 43 remains a proposal as of cutoff.
3. Work authorization — likely REVISE: granted permit rights differ from pending biometric-stage work; §1 and §2 and military criteria matter.
4. Salary/minimum wage — likely KEEP if Compare states no general statutory national minimum; revise any fixed universal figure because wage floors generally come through agreements, while immigration thresholds are route-specific.
5. Ordinary residence after Special Act — likely REVISE if Compare implies automatic conversion; scheme conditions and application/permit basis remain distinct.

| DK-HOUSING-01-C010 | DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities | Municipal assignment and accommodation are separate questions. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `4d779e3b-6659-417c-91ab-6e2dcacd9c05`, `771e221f-dbe5-423b-995c-196ef247f361`, `ce1d50d7-9177-4e11-ada4-7475053a7c2f`, `ee40a28c-8be0-4471-8336-aab8ce453f0d` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-HOUSING-01-C011 | DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities | SIRI’s general FAQ says that Special Act permit holders are covered by section 12 of the Integration Act: a municipality must designate temporary accommodation or may instead designate housing. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `4d779e3b-6659-417c-91ab-6e2dcacd9c05`, `771e221f-dbe5-423b-995c-196ef247f361`, `ce1d50d7-9177-4e11-ada4-7475053a7c2f`, `ee40a28c-8be0-4471-8336-aab8ce453f0d` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-HOUSING-01-C012 | DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities | However, section 57(5) of the Integration Act specifically says that a person granted a permit under section 2(1) of the Ukraine Special Act is not to be assigned temporary accommodation or housing under section 12. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `4d779e3b-6659-417c-91ab-6e2dcacd9c05`, `771e221f-dbe5-423b-995c-196ef247f361`, `ce1d50d7-9177-4e11-ada4-7475053a7c2f`, `ee40a28c-8be0-4471-8336-aab8ce453f0d` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-HOUSING-01-C013 | DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities | The Danish Immigration Service may decide otherwise where very special personal circumstances exist. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `4d779e3b-6659-417c-91ab-6e2dcacd9c05`, `771e221f-dbe5-423b-995c-196ef247f361`, `ce1d50d7-9177-4e11-ada4-7475053a7c2f`, `ee40a28c-8be0-4471-8336-aab8ce453f0d` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-HOUSING-01-C014 | DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities | SIRI’s general FAQ does not mention this exception, so its broader wording does not explain how section 57(5) applies. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `4d779e3b-6659-417c-91ab-6e2dcacd9c05`, `771e221f-dbe5-423b-995c-196ef247f361`, `ce1d50d7-9177-4e11-ada4-7475053a7c2f`, `ee40a28c-8be0-4471-8336-aab8ce453f0d` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-HOUSING-01-C015 | DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities | Do not assume that every permit holder is entitled to municipal accommodation or that this exception automatically removes other municipality-assignment or integration responsibilities. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `4d779e3b-6659-417c-91ab-6e2dcacd9c05`, `771e221f-dbe5-423b-995c-196ef247f361`, `ce1d50d7-9177-4e11-ada4-7475053a7c2f`, `ee40a28c-8be0-4471-8336-aab8ce453f0d` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-HOUSING-01-C016 | DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities | Where the ordinary section 12 rule applies, the municipality must designate temporary accommodation, but it may instead designate housing; a particular apartment, permanent home, or municipality of your choice is not guaranteed. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `4d779e3b-6659-417c-91ab-6e2dcacd9c05`, `771e221f-dbe5-423b-995c-196ef247f361`, `ce1d50d7-9177-4e11-ada4-7475053a7c2f`, `ee40a28c-8be0-4471-8336-aab8ce453f0d` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-HOUSING-01-C017 | DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities | Separately, where section 30(2) of the Ukraine Special Act applies, integration responsibility generally transfers to the municipality no later than 15 working days after the assignment decision; it may transfer earlier by agreement. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `4d779e3b-6659-417c-91ab-6e2dcacd9c05`, `771e221f-dbe5-423b-995c-196ef247f361`, `ce1d50d7-9177-4e11-ada4-7475053a7c2f`, `ee40a28c-8be0-4471-8336-aab8ce453f0d` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-HOUSING-01-C018 | DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities | This timing rule does not itself create an entitlement to accommodation. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `4d779e3b-6659-417c-91ab-6e2dcacd9c05`, `771e221f-dbe5-423b-995c-196ef247f361`, `ce1d50d7-9177-4e11-ada4-7475053a7c2f`, `ee40a28c-8be0-4471-8336-aab8ce453f0d` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |

| DK-EDU-01-C048 | DK-EDU-01 — Costs, financial support and where to apply | Public folkeskole education is free, but private-school, childcare, language-course, adult-learning and higher-education costs differ. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `12b2a6e7-a5c4-45e3-aa7b-31771977b602`, `186153a2-5805-43d8-b615-85eb0c64a256`, `55c3e79d-1c9f-43fe-b01f-b0c0787571a4`, `fcb08b16-c39a-4e68-9a7e-25ce55571450` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-EDU-01-C049 | DK-EDU-01 — Costs, financial support and where to apply | Contact the municipality about a child’s school placement or childcare registration, waiting lists, local fees and possible subsidies. - Ask a school or youth guidance service about upper-secondary and vocational study options. - Ask the municipality or language centre to confirm Danish-course eligibility, placement, and any fee or deposit before enrolling. - Contact a VUC about AVU assessment, subjects and current fees. - Contact the chosen institution about admission, programme requirements and tuition. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `12b2a6e7-a5c4-45e3-aa7b-31771977b602`, `186153a2-5805-43d8-b615-85eb0c64a256`, `55c3e79d-1c9f-43fe-b01f-b0c0787571a4`, `fcb08b16-c39a-4e68-9a7e-25ce55571450` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-EDU-01-C050 | DK-EDU-01 — Costs, financial support and where to apply | The Danish Agency for Higher Education and Science can assess completed foreign qualifications, but the institution decides admission. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `12b2a6e7-a5c4-45e3-aa7b-31771977b602`, `186153a2-5805-43d8-b615-85eb0c64a256`, `55c3e79d-1c9f-43fe-b01f-b0c0787571a4`, `fcb08b16-c39a-4e68-9a7e-25ce55571450` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-EDU-01-C051 | DK-EDU-01 — Costs, financial support and where to apply | SU (the Danish State Educational Grant) is not automatic for foreign citizens or Ukraine Special Act permit holders. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `186153a2-5805-43d8-b615-85eb0c64a256` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-EDU-01-C052 | DK-EDU-01 — Costs, financial support and where to apply | A person covered by the Special Act may apply for equal status under section 2(2) of the Integration Act, but must also meet the general SU conditions, including admission to an SU-approved programme. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `186153a2-5805-43d8-b615-85eb0c64a256` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-EDU-01-C053 | DK-EDU-01 — Costs, financial support and where to apply | Contact the educational institution about an application and the relevant status assessment. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `186153a2-5805-43d8-b615-85eb0c64a256` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C001 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | Waiting for a decision under Denmark’s Ukraine Special Act is not the same as holding a residence permit and does not by itself establish entitlement to cash assistance. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C002 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | SIRI says that a person in a pending procedure cannot receive support under the general Active Social Policy rules while an immigration authority has a duty to maintain that person. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C003 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | If no such duty applies, the other legal conditions must still be met. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C004 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | While an application is pending, the Danish Immigration Service says an applicant may stay in an asylum centre, but this is not required; private accommodation, for example with family or friends, is also possible. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C005 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | Ask the Danish Immigration Service or the authority responsible for your accommodation which body handles your support. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C006 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | A person holding a valid Special Act residence permit may apply to the municipality for cash assistance if they cannot support themselves and meet the other conditions. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C007 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | If the immigration authorities still cover maintenance because the person is accommodated in a reception centre, the route may differ until responsibility is transferred to the municipality. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C008 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | An application does not guarantee payment: the municipality assesses each case individually. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C009 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | Other people living in Denmark are assessed under the general social-assistance rules in light of their residence situation and any other benefits or maintenance available to them. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C010 | DK-SOC-01 — Who can ask for assistance | If you are unsure which authority is responsible, ask your municipality and the authority handling your residence case. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C011 | DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs | The main cash-assistance benefit is called kontanthjælp. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C012 | DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs | Since the reform that took effect on 1 July 2025, this is the name used for assistance in the cash-benefit system. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C013 | DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs | It may be considered when a change in circumstances, such as illness, unemployment or the end of a relationship, leaves a person unable to meet necessary needs for themselves or their family and no other benefit covers that need. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C014 | DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs | General residence and previous-work requirements apply to certain rates. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C015 | DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs | A spouse’s income and assets may also be considered. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C016 | DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs | Separate provisions can apply to people under the Ukraine Special Act, so the municipality must determine which rules and rate apply to an individual case. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C017 | DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs | A separate rule may apply to some married holders of this permit who support their shared minor children in Denmark, if their spouse remains in Ukraine and cannot contribute, fully or partly, to supporting the family. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C018 | DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs | If the conditions are met, assistance may be calculated under rules comparable to those for a single parent. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C019 | DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs | This does not apply automatically just because spouses live apart; the assets of both spouses may still be considered. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C020 | DK-SOC-01 — Benefits for families with children | Child and youth benefit (børne- og ungeydelse) and child allowance in specific circumstances (børnetilskud) are different benefits. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C021 | DK-SOC-01 — Benefits for families with children | Ukrainian citizenship or a Special Act permit alone does not confirm eligibility. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C022 | DK-SOC-01 — Benefits for families with children | Ask Udbetaling Danmark which conditions and application route apply to your family. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C023 | DK-SOC-01 — Benefits for families with children | Certain child allowances may be available to people holding a Special Act permit if the other conditions are met. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C024 | DK-SOC-01 — Benefits for families with children | The rules depend on the type of allowance and family situation, so ask Udbetaling Danmark to assess your case. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C025 | DK-SOC-01 — Benefits for families with children | The municipality may also consider an income-based subsidy towards childcare costs. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C026 | DK-SOC-01 — Benefits for families with children | This is a separate application and is not an automatic addition to kontanthjælp. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C027 | DK-SOC-01 — Help with certain necessary expenses | A municipality may individually consider help with certain reasonable and necessary expenses, for example an unforeseen expense after a change in circumstances, some health or dental expenses, or moving costs. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C028 | DK-SOC-01 — Help with certain necessary expenses | Separate provisions under integration legislation may apply to people covered by the Ukraine Special Act. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C029 | DK-SOC-01 — Help with certain necessary expenses | The applicable route depends on the person’s status and circumstances. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C030 | DK-SOC-01 — Help with certain necessary expenses | Help is not automatic. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C031 | DK-SOC-01 — Help with certain necessary expenses | The municipality considers whether the expense is necessary, the person’s income and savings, family situation, and whether another rule covers the cost. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C032 | DK-SOC-01 — Help with certain necessary expenses | You should normally contact the municipality before incurring the expense. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C033 | DK-SOC-01 — Help with certain necessary expenses | Detailed housing-benefit and deposit-loan guidance is covered separately in the Denmark × Housing article. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C034 | DK-SOC-01 — Work, activities and reporting changes | If you receive kontanthjælp, the municipality may assess whether you need to be available for work, search for jobs or take part in activities. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C035 | DK-SOC-01 — Work, activities and reporting changes | Requirements depend on an individual assessment and may take health or other circumstances into account. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C036 | DK-SOC-01 — Work, activities and reporting changes | Failing to meet a requirement without a reasonable reason can affect payments. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C037 | DK-SOC-01 — Work, activities and reporting changes | As of 17 November 2026, people holding a Ukraine Special Act permit are not covered by the separate arbejdspligt regime. | UPDATED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Refresh the as-of date; the official current guidance still supports the bounded claim. |
| DK-SOC-01-C038 | DK-SOC-01 — Work, activities and reporting changes | This exemption is specific to arbejdspligt; it does not remove other integration-programme requirements, individual appointments or benefit conditions. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C039 | DK-SOC-01 — Work, activities and reporting changes | If you receive assistance, tell the municipality about changes that may affect eligibility or the amount, such as changes in income or household circumstances. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |

| DK-SOC-01-C041 | DK-SOC-01 — How to apply | To ask about kontanthjælp, start with the municipality where you live or the municipality responsible for your case. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |
| DK-SOC-01-C042 | DK-SOC-01 — How to apply | The general application is made through the municipality or its digital service. | CONFIRMED | SourceId evidence: `675030ce-d243-48a2-a548-d1c2ae7ca323`, `b64fed15-d770-4a60-a908-c4cfb8b4df6b`, `bf5a5cb2-f724-4957-9af7-7a091903e365` | As stated in proposition; as of 17 Nov 2026 otherwise | Retain; keep the stated qualification and route limits. |

### Part 2 numeric/date audit

Numeric/date-bearing proposition sentences audited: 20/20. Combined with Part 1: 62/62. Repeated values across articles are counted once per article-specific claim; source identifiers are excluded.

- **DK-HEALTH-01 — The yellow health card (sundhedskort):** Delivery generally takes several weeks.
- **DK-HEALTH-01 — Interpretation and language support:** A fee may apply for language-related interpretation if you have lived in Denmark for more than three years; exceptions are listed in the official guidance.
- **DK-HEALTH-01 — Costs and coverage:** Under group 1, treatment with a GP or specialist who has an agreement with the region is free; referrals are required for some services.
- **DK-HEALTH-01 — Costs and coverage:** Under group 2, you may have to pay the difference if the provider’s price is higher than the regional subsidy.
- **DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities:** SIRI’s general FAQ says that Special Act permit holders are covered by section 12 of the Integration Act: a municipality must designate temporary accommodation or may instead designate housing.
- **DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities:** However, section 57(5) of the Integration Act specifically says that a person granted a permit under section 2(1) of the Ukraine Special Act is not to be assigned temporary accommodation or housing under section 12.
- **DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities:** SIRI’s general FAQ does not mention this exception, so its broader wording does not explain how section 57(5) applies.
- **DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities:** Where the ordinary section 12 rule applies, the municipality must designate temporary accommodation, but it may instead designate housing; a particular apartment, permanent home, or municipality of your choice is not guaranteed.
- **DK-HOUSING-01 — Accommodation through municipalities:** Separately, where section 30(2) of the Ukraine Special Act applies, integration responsibility generally transfers to the municipality no later than 15 working days after the assignment decision; it may transfer earlier by agreement.
- **DK-HOUSING-01 — Housing-related financial support:** The rules include people with permits under sections 1 and 2 of the Ukraine Special Act who apply within the first three years after receiving the permit, subject to conditions.
- **DK-HOUSING-01 — Moving and address registration:** You can normally report it from four weeks before moving and must do so no later than five days after the move.
- **DK-EDU-01 — Schools for children:** Compulsory education normally covers ten years, including grade 0 (børnehaveklasse) and grades 1–9; the obligation is to receive education, not necessarily to attend a municipal school.
- **DK-EDU-01 — Nurseries and kindergartens:** Children whose parents have legal residence in Denmark are covered by the childcare guarantee from 26 weeks of age until school starts.
- **DK-EDU-01 — Danish language and adult learning:** Adults over 25 can ask a VUC adult education centre about general adult education (avu), which offers subjects at levels corresponding to grades 9 and 10.
- **DK-EDU-01 — Higher education and qualifications:** This assessment is free and normally takes one to two months after the required documents are submitted.
- **DK-EDU-01 — Costs, financial support and where to apply:** A person covered by the Special Act may apply for equal status under section 2(2) of the Integration Act, but must also meet the general SU conditions, including admission to an SU-approved programme.
- **DK-SOC-01 — Cash assistance for necessary living costs:** Since the reform that took effect on 1 July 2025, this is the name used for assistance in the cash-benefit system.
- **DK-SOC-01 — Work, activities and reporting changes:** As of 17 November 2026, people holding a Ukraine Special Act permit are not covered by the separate arbejdspligt regime.
- **DK-SOC-01 — How to apply:** A complaint about a municipal kontanthjælp decision is submitted to the municipality within four weeks of receiving the decision.
- **DK-SOC-01 — How to apply:** A Udbetaling Danmark family-benefit decision has a separate process; generally, the agency must receive a complaint within four weeks.

### Healthcare findings

For a permit holder, the Ministry’s Ukraine-specific guidance describes all Health Act services once CPR registration and issue of the yellow health card are in place. Permit, CPR registration and card are separate steps; the municipality handles CPR/card administration, regions organize health services, and the GP is the first routine contact under group 1. Hospital treatment after referral is covered for people in the public scheme; group 2 may require payment above the regional subsidy. Medicines and dental care may involve charges or subsidy rules; do not call all Danish healthcare free. Interpretation may be chargeable after more than three years in Denmark, subject to exceptions. Urgent/emergency routes and numbers differ by region except 112 for life-threatening emergencies.

Pending applicants have narrower access. Current Danish Immigration Service guidance says applicants who can show proof of their application have access to several health services. The 2022 Ministry notice described the original arrangement and is retained as historical context; current wording does not treat its acute/continuing-care categories as the operative rule. This does not establish routine GP access, full public insurance, ordinary medicine reimbursement or universal healthcare coverage for every pending applicant. The current article remains explicitly scoped to permit holders and does not assert that pending applicants have no care.

### Housing findings

DIS guidance permits applicants covered by its waiting framework to stay in an asylum centre or privately; this is not a guarantee of publicly funded accommodation. Municipal lodging/meals is a discretionary route subject to filing/status and other-support conditions; it may continue for a permit holder until municipal responsibility transfer. Municipal allocation, integration responsibility and housing placement are separate. Integration Act §57(5) specifically excludes §2(1) Ukraine Special Act permits from ordinary §12 placement, subject to a very-special-personal-circumstances decision; other municipal duties are not removed by that exception. Where §12 applies, temporary accommodation or a housing placement is within the statutory scheme, not a promise of a chosen home or municipality. Transfer deadlines are separate from an accommodation entitlement. Housing benefit (boligstøtte) is a current application route through Udbetaling Danmark and is assessed using residence, dwelling and household factors; a deposit loan is a separate current municipal route with eligibility conditions. Individually assessed moving/rent help is local and discretionary. No closed emergency scheme is presented as a current entitlement. Private tenancy law guidance covers leases, deposit/prepaid rent distinction, defect reporting and complaint boards; the draft states no universal rent/deposit cap. Individual expense help is discretionary.

### Education findings

Compulsory education is an obligation to receive education, generally from six until completion (usually 16); it is not a requirement to attend municipal folkeskole specifically. Municipality assesses displaced children individually and handles placement/language support. Ukraine-specific temporary flexibility ended 17 March 2026; current UVM guidance confirms a separate legal basis for some virtual/English/Ukrainian instruction remains, subject to local pedagogical assessment and Danish instruction requirements. Displaced pupils are not required to attend Ukrainian remote school. Home education is a separate choice: written notice before commencement and municipal supervision apply.

Daycare guarantee applies to children with parents legally resident in Denmark, from 26 weeks until school start, under standard rules; it does not guarantee a named facility or immediate placement. Fees and subsidies depend on statutory rules, household income and municipality. Ukraine-specific daycare exceptions expired 17 March 2026. Upper-secondary and VET admission is institution-specific and subject to usual or adjusted statutory admission rules; higher education is programme-specific. UCPH tuition/application deposit exemption is institution-specific. SU equal status is an application route for eligible §2(2) cases, not automatic payment. Foreign qualification assessment explains level but does not itself grant admission.

### Social and benefits findings

Current general cash assistance is called kontanthjælp after the reform effective 1 July 2025; older benefit labels/rates should not be presented as current. A permit holder may apply where unable to support themselves and other conditions apply; municipality decides. Reception-centre maintenance can change which authority is responsible. Rates depend on age, residence/work history, income/assets and sometimes spouse resources. A narrow Special Act §30c rule can affect assistance calculation for some married permit holders whose spouse remains in Ukraine and cannot contribute, and requires evidence/municipal assessment; this is not automatic based on separation. Child and youth benefit (børne- og ungeydelse), child allowance (børnetilskud), childcare subsidy and kontanthjælp child supplements are distinct. Udbetaling Danmark handles family benefits; municipalities decide cash assistance and individually assessed expenses. Housing benefit is covered separately. Unemployment insurance and municipal assistance are separate; the article directs jobseekers to the employment article rather than promising benefits. As of 17 Nov 2026 L 43 remains a bill referred to committee, so the current arbetspligt exception for Special Act permit holders remains in force; proposed changes are not current law.

### High-risk evidence finding

The current official Immigration Service notice “Extension of residence permit under the Ukraine-Special Act,” published 29 October 2026, was located and opened at [nyidanmark.dk](https://www.nyidanmark.dk/en-GB/News-Front-Page/2026/10/Extension-of-residence-permit-under-the-Ukraine-Special-Act). It separately confirms the extension through 17 March 2028 subject to continued eligibility, automatic assessment, no individual application, and same rights while the extension case remains pending past 17 March 2027. The current Ukraine information page at [nyidanmark.dk](https://www.nyidanmark.dk/en-GB/Words-and-concepts/F%C3%A6lles/Information-Ukraine) gives the detailed ongoing requirements. BEK 828/2026 remains the legal instrument. These findings support the Task 85 protection-date candidate, but the 29 October notice is not represented by a distinct existing source record and must be proposed as an ADD with an explicit SourceId before final Compare/article binding audit.

The production source inventory contains two separate owned BEK 828/2026 records (`f947d1e6-c475-4676-844b-dff215001a87` and `886dfd95-7713-4c69-9b3f-a2d3be8ad323`), and separate owned duplicates of current Special Act guidance across articles. This is an owner+URL duplicate cleanup candidate, not an automatic deletion: current article bindings must be preserved or migrated and the exact 102-row audit must disposition each record first.

### Remaining work / stop condition

Task 86 cannot be marked complete from this pass: the 102 source records still require individual substantive-scope, claim-ID, current/legacy, and action assignments; 49 unreferenced records have no final disposition; a complete 372-claim coverage map and the 24-row Compare binding decision have not been finished; and the missing 29 October notice record needs a stable proposed ID and complete metadata. No review-copy wording or production/canonical data was changed. This is a source-audit evidence gap, not evidence that any approved candidate claim is wrong; no `CLAIM REOPEN REQUIRED` finding is made.

## Task 88 — final 372-claim coverage map

This completed Task 88 map supersedes earlier “not complete” and “blocked” status notes in Tasks 86–87; those notes described the state before this row-level mapping and binding pass.

**As of:** 17 November 2026. This row-level map applies Task 85’s approved propositions without re-atomizing them. Rows marked supporting are practical, navigational, or limiting statements. For every externally factual/legal proposition, SourceIds identify the audited source or fully specified ADD record used for the stated role. The 43 entries marked NOT EXTERNALLY SOURCE-DEPENDENT are advice, article-scope caveats, or logical/navigation statements rather than standalone legal facts.

| Claim ID | Article | Status | Materiality | Final candidate proposition | SourceId(s) | Evidence role | Coverage result |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| DK-TP-01-C001 | DK-TP-01 | CONFIRMED | MATERIAL | Denmark’s Ukraine Special Act is a national scheme. | `441b7bd9-54cf-47a8-80a1-e89a0e410952`, `486ee6fc-fc67-4d04-891b-acb967e5477b` | Retsinformation, “Act on temporary residence permits for persons displaced from Ukraine” (Act no. 324 of 16 Mar 2022); Danish Immigration Service, “Apply for a residence permit as a person in Denmark displaced from Ukraine” | COVERED — MULTIPLE SOURCES |
| DK-TP-01-C002 | DK-TP-01 | CONFIRMED | MATERIAL | Denmark does not participate in the EU Temporary Protection Directive. | `441b7bd9-54cf-47a8-80a1-e89a0e410952`, `6677f201-1246-417c-8d8f-56dc99c93b0f` | Retsinformation, “Act on temporary residence permits for persons displaced from Ukraine” (Act no. 324 of 16 Mar 2022); EUR-Lex, “Council Directive 2001/55/EC” | COVERED — MULTIPLE SOURCES |
| DK-TP-01-C003 | DK-TP-01 | CONFIRMED | MATERIAL | A protection permit or status from another EU country does not by itself establish eligibility under Danish law. | `441b7bd9-54cf-47a8-80a1-e89a0e410952`, `486ee6fc-fc67-4d04-891b-acb967e5477b` | Retsinformation, “Act on temporary residence permits for persons displaced from Ukraine” (Act no. 324 of 16 Mar 2022); Danish Immigration Service, “Apply for a residence permit as a person in Denmark displaced from Ukraine” | COVERED — MULTIPLE SOURCES |
| DK-TP-01-C004 | DK-TP-01 | CONFIRMED | MATERIAL | The [[source:486ee6fc-fc67-4d04-891b-acb967e5477b/Danish Immigration Service]] says a person may qualify under section 1 if, for example, they are a Ukrainian citizen who left Ukraine on or after 1 February 2022 and had residence there on or after that date; a person recognised as a refugee in Ukraine on 24 February 2022 who left Ukraine on or after 1 February 2022; or a Ukrainian citizen or such a recognised refugee who was in Denmark or held Danish residence permission on 24 February 2022. | `441b7bd9-54cf-47a8-80a1-e89a0e410952`, `486ee6fc-fc67-4d04-891b-acb967e5477b` | Retsinformation, “Act on temporary residence permits for persons displaced from Ukraine” (Act no. 324 of 16 Mar 2022); Danish Immigration Service, “Apply for a residence permit as a person in Denmark displaced from Ukraine” | COVERED — MULTIPLE SOURCES |

### Inventory reconciliation and exceptions

The 102 Denmark-owned records reconcile to the Task 88 active set of 69, plus 33 records initially labelled unreferenced. The binding audit found six of those 33 referenced in `step-by-step-guides.json`; the final 372-claim map also uses one additional record, `661ee691-3e83-4200-8360-58a197cb81a4`, for DK-EMP-02-C036/C037/C038. Per Task 89, cleanup classification stopped for these seven records; each is moved into active-source accounting as CURRENT / KEEP. The six guide-bound records have ten binding instances in total. Therefore the reconciled active existing inventory is **76** (69 Task 88 Core/Compare evidence records + 6 guide-bound records + 1 Task 88 map dependency), and the remaining truly unreferenced set is **26**.
